# Agentic LLM ranking

Generated by `.claude/skills/amg-llm-bench/scripts/rank_llms.py`.
Reference model: `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`
(relative price and relative cost per task are 1.00 there).

Columns, in plain words:

- **capability per cost** — how good the model is, set against what
  it costs: the mean of three equal thirds, each min-max scaled over
  the plotted variants.
- **raw capability (0-100)** — how well the model suits this group's
  work, from the group's own benchmark weights.
- **preference** — the same fit run over this group's Arena boards
  alone — blind human votes; `-` where the row carries no Arena board
  here at all. The raw capability is the mean of the pillar columns.
- **capability** — the same fit over the group's Artificial Analysis
  boards alone. A wide gap between the two pillar columns is a
  finding about the model, not an error.
- **effective cost (x reference)** — the geometric mean of relative
  list price and relative cost per task, as a multiple of the
  reference model.
- **evidence coverage** — the share of the group's benchmark weight
  this variant was measured on, at its own reasoning effort. Nothing
  else is behind the row: an unmeasured board is left out, not filled
  in.
- **measured boards** — how many of the group's boards this variant
  has a measured row on, out of how many the group weighs.
- **ability band** — the standard error of this row's ability in the
  least-squares fit: how far the raw capability could move on the
  evidence behind it. It narrows with boards measured, never with
  boards borrowed.

**Nothing here is copied, scaled or filled in.** A variant is
scored on the boards it was itself measured on, at its own
reasoning effort, and on no others. No score moves across
efforts or across models, and a model no same-source ratio
reaches has no cost per task at all rather than a guessed one.

**The boards are sparse, so the scores are solved, not
averaged.** Per group,
`value(variant, board) = board level + ability` is fitted by
weighted least squares over exactly the cells that exist, each
cell weighted by its board's weight in the group. The board
level absorbs how hard or how generous a board is; the ability
is what is left, and that is the raw capability printed here.
Two variants are compared through the boards they share —
which boards those are differs per pair, and the fit uses
whichever they turn out to be.

**Evidence coverage** is the share of the group's benchmark
weight measured on a row, and the **ability band** is the
standard error the fit puts on it: a row on three boards is
printed with a wide band, not with somebody else's number. A
row measured on fewer than 3 of a group's
boards is not scored in that group on its own boards; it is
scored only if another reasoning effort of the same model was
measured on enough of the group and the two share a board, and
otherwise it is named in the *not scored in this group* list
under the group. A row also has to have been measured itself,
at its own effort, on enough of the group's boards and enough
of its weight before it may sit on a frontier; one that has
not says *too thin for the frontier* and names the bar it fell
under. Nothing is inherited there, so an anchor can hold a
line its placed siblings do not.

Prices are paid list prices. A `:free` listing is rate-limited
and temporary, so it is never a price: free listings are priced
by the same model's paid listing, and a model with no paid
listing anywhere is out of the paid ranking altogether.

**Every row is a model at one reasoning effort**, named
`Model (effort)`, because low effort and max effort are two
different buys. 237 variants over
166 models: an effort gets its own row
where a board published a row for it or a run published its cost
multiplier, and `(default)` means no source named an effort at
all. Effort multiplies the cost per task and never the list
price. A board with no row at that effort is simply a board
this variant is not scored on.

**Effort is only priced where somebody published the
multiplier.** 28 of those 109 variants
sit on a measured multiplier. The rest have no cost per task at
this effort — the default effort's cost is not this effort's
cost, and running a model at max almost certainly burns more
tokens than running it at low — so their capability per cost is
the list-price and raw-capability thirds alone and their
effective cost on the charts is the list price.

Status words:

- **on frontier** — nothing is both cheaper and a better fit.
- **off frontier** — something else is cheaper and fits better at
  once.
- **too thin for the frontier** — this row was not itself measured on
  enough of the group to be allowed on the frontier, and the status
  says which bar it fell under: the boards of its own it was run on,
  the share of the group's weight behind it, or both. Nothing is
  inherited — an effort placed against a better-measured sibling is
  judged on what was run at its own effort. It is still scored,
  ranked, plotted and listed among the least-measured models; a row
  nobody has really measured simply cannot set the line the rest of
  the page is read against.
- **price-only, no cost-per-task data** — no same-source ratio
  reaches this model, so it has no cost per task at all. Nothing is
  invented for it: its capability per cost is the list-price and
  raw-capability thirds alone, and its effective cost on the chart is
  its list price.
- **no cost-per-task data at this reasoning effort** — the model is
  priced per task but nobody published a cost multiplier for this
  effort, and the default effort's cost is not this effort's cost.
  The row is treated as having none.
- **pillar missing** — nobody measured this row on any board of that
  pillar, so the column is blank and the raw capability is the pillar
  that remains — which means a different thing from a mean over both,
  and says so. A row missing a pillar is not ranked at all unless
  another effort of the same model carries it.
- **effort cost measured** — a published run priced this model at
  this reasoning effort against another effort of itself, so the cost
  on this row is the effort's own cost.
- **placed relative to** — this row is an effort variant of a model
  whose best-measured variant sits on more boards, so each of its
  pillars is that variant's pillar plus the weighted mean gap between
  the two on the boards of that pillar both were measured on. A
  pillar they share no board in keeps the anchor's value: a lead on
  Arena is a statement about preference and about nothing else. A
  global fit has no model-by-board term, so a family-wide weakness on
  the boards only one variant was run on would otherwise be charged
  to that variant alone.
- **no board shared with** — this effort variant and its family's
  best-measured variant were never run on the same board, so there is
  no distance between the two to read. It keeps whatever the group's
  own fit gave it, and gets no number at all if the fit never reached
  it.
- **free listing exists** — the same model also has a free listing on
  the same id, with a :free suffix. That lane is throttled and
  temporary, so the ranking still prices the model at its paid list
  price.

Every number behind these tables — each benchmark, its weight,
its raw and normalised scores, every cost ratio and every list
price — is in `.claude/skills/amg-llm-bench/results/evidence.md`
and on the frontier page.

## Cost basis

List price is one blended number per model: the price of a
million input tokens and the price of a million output tokens,
averaged with output counted 3 times as heavily as
input.

That output weighting is **assumed**. Agentic traffic is
output-dominated once prompt caching works, but nothing in this
skill measures the ratio: no pipeline step records an output-token
median. It is a single number to change, `price_blend:` in
`.claude/skills/amg-llm-bench/data/task_groups.yaml`, and every price here moves
with it.

Relative price and relative cost per task are both multiples of
`deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`; effective cost is their geometric mean.

**The `rung:` labels are two ladders, not one.**

- `r1`-`r22` — the ladder the presets walk, one
  model per rung. It is placed by hand in `models.yaml` and runs
  in roughly ascending list price, so it is a slot rather than a
  computed rank: read the blended price beside it, not the
  number. A rung held by a rejected model records where that
  model would sit; the ranking still excludes it.
- `claude-easy`, `claude-medium` — the Claude-only
  preset's difficulty tiers, which are not positions on the
  ladder above. That preset is pinned to one provider, so its
  tiers are named rather than numbered.

## coding-experiments

Steps: `execute.experiment`, `execute.dataset`, `execute.evaluation`, `gen_viz`, `gen_demo_art`

Two pillars at capability 50%, preference 50% of
the group's weight. The raw capability is the mean of the
pillar scores this row has, so a row missing a pillar is
scored over the one it carries and its status says which
one is gone.

Pending: Arena's two agentic boards are the only human read of agent behaviour anywhere in this file, and they cover a fraction of the pool; until that widens the preference half here leans on the WebDev and coding Elos, which are votes about text. Nothing in the capability half scores a patch against a real repository's own test suite any more — SWE-bench went with the hand-transcribed boards — so what is left reads terminal and tool behaviour instead. A reproducible SWE-bench capture is the single biggest thing this group is missing.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

## research-reasoning

Steps: `gen_hypo`, `review_hypo`, `upd_hypo`, `execute.research`, `execute.proof`

Two pillars at capability 50%, preference 50% of
the group's weight. The raw capability is the mean of the
pillar scores this row has, so a row missing a pillar is
scored over the one it carries and its status says which
one is gone.

Pending: Nothing here reads retrieval as a re-run task: the search boards that exist run on a handful of models each, which is an anecdote wearing a board's clothes, so search is read through Arena's search Elo alone. GPQA is close to saturated across the models anyone is actually choosing between, and CritPt reads one discipline. Nothing here measures whether a hypothesis was any good, which is the thing the step is for; the boards measure whether the model knows things and can find things.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

## scientific-writing

Steps: `gen_paper_text`, `gen_full_paper`

Two pillars at capability 50%, preference 50% of
the group's weight. The raw capability is the mean of the
pillar scores this row has, so a row missing a pillar is
scored over the one it carries and its status says which
one is gone.

Pending: No benchmark measuring scientific or academic paper writing covers this pool, so the capability half is long-context reasoning and constraint-following standing in for prose quality and one document-production board standing in for the artifact. Nothing in the group measures claim accuracy against a source, citation faithfulness or revision to reviewer comments. Read it as general writing preference held to a brief until such a board exists. `aa_aabriefcasetimepertask` was considered for this half and left out: it is a duration, not a quality, and a model that is fast at a briefcase task is not thereby better at writing a paper.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

### 38 least-measured models (scientific-writing)

The ranked rows with the least of this group's benchmark
weight behind them. They are ranked and plotted like any
other, on their own boards — or, where the status says
*placed relative to*, at a distance from another effort
of the same model — and their bands are the widest here.

were kept off the frontier for that reason.

## review-judging

Steps: `review_paper`

Two pillars at capability 50%, preference 50% of
the group's weight. The raw capability is the mean of the
pillar scores this row has, so a row missing a pillar is
scored over the one it carries and its status says which
one is gone.

Pending: Judging is the step with the least direct evidence anywhere in this file: no board here scores a review against what a conference's own reviewers said. The Omniscience Index is the closest thing to "would it notice the claim is wrong", because it scores confident error negative rather than ignoring it. Until a review board exists this group is a proxy stack, and the `pillar missing` marks on thin rows are the honest part of it.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

## general

Steps: `gen_strat`, `gen_plan.*`

Two pillars at capability 50%, preference 50% of
the group's weight. The raw capability is the mean of the
pillar scores this row has, so a row missing a pillar is
scored over the one it carries and its status says which
one is gone.

Pending: A union is a stand-in for a measurement nobody has taken: no board anywhere in this file scores whether a plan was a good plan, and the steps here are the cheapest in the pipeline and the ones every later step inherits. The half shares the other groups gave their thinnest boards are dropped here — fourteen capability boards at one fourteenth each already hold any one of them to under 4% of the group — so a thin board moves this group only through the rows that have it. Read the ordering as "good at everything", which is what a planning step asks for and also the vaguest thing this file can say.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

### 108 models not scored in this group (general)

These carry a board here and still cannot be read on
their own: fewer than 3 of the
group's boards at their own reasoning effort, or boards
in only one of the two pillars. No effort of the same
model shares a board with them either, so there is
nothing to place them against. They are not scored,
ranked or plotted here, and no number is invented for
them. The boards they do have are on the evidence page.

## overall

Mean of every group's raw-capability score.

Frontier rows first, then the rest by raw capability --
the axis the frontier is drawn on, so no row here sits above
one that beats it on both cost and capability. Capability
per cost is a column to read, not the order. Read the band
beside every raw capability too: it is the standard error of
the ability in this group's fit, so a row measured on three
boards carries a wide one.

## Free pool

The free backend needs an ordering of `:free` listings, and
that is all this table is. A `:free` listing is not a price: it
is a shared rate-limit pool that throttles, queues and lapses,
so **free availability is unreliable** and these rows are
ordered by raw capability alone, never by cost.

A slug takes its raw capability straight from the paid row it
is a free listing of, on the paid scale, because the weights
behind the two endpoints are the same model. The endpoint is
not: each row keeps the free lane's own context window,
response ceiling and tool-calling flag, and those are what a
task group's hard floors are checked against.

**Paid listing** says whether the same model has one. A model
marked `none` is free-only with no list price, so it cannot be
priced, has no base row to inherit from and never enters the
paid ranking or a frontier.

Paid row exists but is not scored anywhere — too few of
the group's boards, or tagged as carrying no evidence — so
there is nothing for the slug to inherit: Laguna S 2.1 (free), Laguna XS 2.1 (free), Nemotron 3 Ultra (free), Nemotron 3 Super 120B A12B (free), Gemma 4 31B IT (free).

Free-only, no list price: Nex N2.5 Pro (free), Nex N2.5 Mini (free), North Mini Code (free).

- **GLM-5.2 (free)** — In the free router's pool. Scored off the paid GLM-5.2 row, but the free endpoint is a different endpoint: 32k of context and no tool calling, which is under every task group's floor.
- **Nex N2.5 Pro (free)** — Free-only: no paid Nex listing exists, so it has no list price and cannot be ranked against paid models. Highest long-horizon evidence in the free pool (TB 2.1 82.7).
- **Nex N2.5 Mini (free)** — Free-only: no paid Nex listing, so no list price. A better terminal driver than Laguna S (TB 2.1 73.4 vs 70.2) and far weaker on SWE-Pro — prefer it when the hard steps are terminal work.
- **Laguna S 2.1 (free)** — Best free patch writer (SWE-Pro 59.4). The same model is listed paid at $0.09/$0.18, and that paid row is what carries it through the ranking; this row exists only to order the free lane.
- **Laguna XS 2.1 (free)** — Weakest free listing; a real-harness KiloBench run put it at 26.7% completion. Listed paid at $0.06/$0.12, which is where its price comes from.
- **Nemotron 3 Ultra (free)** — In the free router's pool, and the widest free window in it at 1M. Scored off the paid Nemotron 3 Ultra row.
- **Nemotron 3 Super 120B A12B (free)** — In the free router's pool. Its paid row is on no board this skill carries, so it inherits no evidence and is ordered last.
- **Gemma 4 31B IT (free)** — In the free router's pool. Its paid row is on no board this skill carries, so it inherits no evidence and is ordered last.
- **North Mini Code (free)** — In the free router's pool and free-only: the catalogue lists no paid Cohere North Mini Code, so there is no base row to inherit from and no evidence for it anywhere in this skill.

## Priced, no evidence

**78** priced candidates appear on no board this skill
carries, so they have a list price and no raw capability. They are
ranked nowhere, drawn nowhere, and they do not anchor any
scale — a price with no evidence behind it is not a bargain,
it is an unknown. They are listed so the next run can see what
was already screened and what is still waiting for a board.

| GLM 5V Turbo | vision-first model |
| Qwen 3.7 Flash | teaser price, 8.88% tool errors |
| Mercury 2.5 | vendor-only figures |
| GPT-OSS 20B | AA Agentic Index 1.4 |
| GPT-OSS 120B | TB-Hard 23.5, AA Coding 30.4 |
| Nemotron 3 Ultra | 36.5 schema retries per task |
| Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite | strictly dominated |
| MiMo V2.5 | no published scores |
| Mistral Nemo | 2024 12B, endpoint lacks tools |

- **GLM 4.6v** — A `v` line is z.ai's vision-language branch: the scores here are the text boards the same weights happen to sit on, and the model is sold for image and document understanding, not for driving a terminal. Out of scope by the same rule as every other vision-first model (SKILL.md section 1); the rows are kept so the filter is reproducible.
- **GLM 4.5v** — A `v` line is z.ai's vision-language branch: the scores here are the text boards the same weights happen to sit on, and the model is sold for image and document understanding, not for driving a terminal. Out of scope by the same rule as every other vision-first model (SKILL.md section 1); the rows are kept so the filter is reproducible.
- **GLM 5V Turbo** — A `v` line is z.ai's vision-language branch: the scores here are the text boards the same weights happen to sit on, and the model is sold for image and document understanding, not for driving a terminal. Out of scope by the same rule as every other vision-first model (SKILL.md section 1); the rows are kept so the filter is reproducible.
- **Qwen 3.7 Flash** — Vision-first model; the $0.03/$0.13 headline is a sub-32k-prompt teaser (>=32k is $0.10/$0.40, >=256k is $0.20/$0.80, and an agentic subagent always lives above 32k); 8.88% tool-call error rate, about one failure in eleven calls; zero published coding benchmarks (benchlm: "0 of 439", unranked); single provider, no fallback.
- **Mercury 2.5** — Diffusion LLM at ~1,107 tok/s, but no SWE-bench, Terminal-Bench or LiveCodeBench score is published and every figure traces to the vendor's own announcement. On frontier for latency-bound tool dispatch only, unevidenced for editing code.
- **GPT-OSS 20B** — AA Coding Index 20.7 (rank 87 of 102), AA Agentic Index 1.4, GDPval- AA 0.7 — near zero on the agentic boards.
- **GPT-OSS 120B** — LiveCodeBench 87.8 but AA Coding Index 30.4 and Terminal-Bench-Hard 23.5 — strong at writing a snippet, weak at driving a terminal.
- **Nemotron 3 Ultra** — SWE-bench Verified 71.9, but a production harness measured 36.5 structured-output retries per task against a baseline's 0.3. A tool- loop agent cannot absorb that failure rate.
- **Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite** — SWE-bench Pro 54.2 and Terminal-Bench 2.1 54 while costing 2x the input and 5x the output of GLM 5.3 Flash — strictly dominated.
- **MiMo V2.5** — Vendor claims Pro-level agentic performance at half the cost, but the model's own benchmark page publishes no scores and no independent SWE-bench or Terminal-Bench row exists. Unevidenced.
- **Mistral Nemo** — Cheapest tool-capable model in the catalogue, but a 12B dense model created 2024-07-19 with no agentic-coding evidence dated in the window, and its cheapest endpoint does not list `tools`.

## Aliases folded into their snapshots

An undated alias and the dated snapshot it resolves to are one
endpoint, so they are one row: the alias's boards are merged
into the snapshot, the dated row's list price wins, and the
alias is not plotted. Where to look instead:

| alias | scored as |
|---|---|
| deepseek/deepseek-r1 | deepseek/deepseek-r1-0528 |
| deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash | deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731 |
| deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro | deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813 |
| mistralai/mistral-large | mistralai/mistral-large-2407 |
| moonshotai/kimi-k2 | moonshotai/kimi-k2-0905 |

## Preset cuts

One ladder per agent backend per task group, cut four ways.
The rungs are that group's Pareto frontier, weakest first,
restricted to the ids the backend can actually route and to
the rows that clear the group's hard requirements; the free
lane has no frontier, so it is ordered by evidence and then
by context window. A ladder with fewer than five rungs is
left short and the presets clamp onto its top, so no two
tiers are ever handed the same model by padding. Rung
numbers below are for a full ladder. The cuts themselves,
with efforts, free-lane fallbacks and clamp notes, are in
`results/cuts.yaml`.

Every preset reads the same ladder at these rungs:

| preset | easy | medium | hard | lead |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| lite | 1 | 1 | 1 | 2 |
| pro | 1 | 1 | 2 | 3 |
| max | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| ultra | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |

## Best pick per pipeline step

One row per step of the pipeline, picked off that step's own
frontier: the best raw capability inside each band of effective cost.
Three picks rather than one, because the frontier spans two
orders of magnitude of cost and the cheapest frontier row and
the best one are rarely the same buy.

Every step is scored by its group, so the 15 steps
below share 5 scorings and rows
repeat: look your step up rather than reading down.

**Effective cost at or under the reference** (over 0x up to 1x):

| step | best pick | raw capability |
|---|---|---|
| execute.dataset | DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (max) | 60.8 |
| execute.evaluation | DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (max) | 60.8 |
